## Named local-class enclosing names and ABI identity checkpoint

Implementation base HEAD: `52d6fb5960bc57803abf4f203eeca150a7714e77`.
The stage base and last-audited commit above are unchanged. This is a coherent
partial implementation handoff; **PA11 remains incomplete**. The previous goal
turn was **progress**, evidenced by that committed runtime bit-field group;
this turn is **progress**, established by the fixed-manifest reduction below.
Ralph owns acceptance, audits and advancement.

### Completed group and direct C++ owners

Read AGENTS.md, CLONE_CONTRACT.md, spec.md, the conversion plan, PA11 handout
and current ledger; inspected raw protected failures and their first divergent
owners. Translated the named local-class enclosing-name/ABI group. Consulted frozen
`sema/analyzer_lambdas.cpp::InNestedBody/local_name/odr_use_constant_operand`,
`analyzer.h::odr_use_constants`, `analyzer_expressions.cpp`'s designation,
address and conversion consumers, `analyzer_initialization.cpp`'s default owner,
`analyzer_declarations.cpp`'s default-argument context,
`analyzer_classes.cpp::new_class/local_number`,
`lower/local_mangling.cpp::local_discriminator/name_local_classes`,
`symbol_lowering.cpp::abi_scope/class_name/type_locality/entity_locality/
local_signature/unit_local/shared_local`, and `sema/semantics_dump.cpp`'s
keyword/id presentation.

- `sema/local_names.rs` checks lexical scope crossings using ScopeIds and the
  original resolved BindingId. Reading an enclosing const integral or unscoped
  enum constant publishes its bits on the existing ExprId, preserving its source
  TypeId, cv, lvalue category and designation. Parameters and nonconstant
  automatics reject at the original use. Static/extern/TLS objects and
  unevaluated/template contexts keep the C++ exemptions.
- An enclosing-local fold style distinguishes such reads from literals and
  static member constants. Address-of, explicit reference casts and selected
  scalar/reference arguments check actual storage demand. Direct reference
  binding rejects, while a value conversion followed by temporary binding
  remains valid. Comma/conditional potential results follow the original typed
  children, including their diagnostic order, without name lookup or reparsing.
  A provisional designation is published before constant reference evaluation,
  matching the oracle's new_facts ownership.
- Immediate default arguments retain the declaration's local-name restriction;
  deferred complete-class defaults run with the restored surrounding context.
  Local-class defaults and member initializers can read enclosing constants
  while storage uses retain the exact local-class diagnostic.
- `sema/local_classes.rs` privately owns declaration counters, saturated u16
  ordinals, and outer-function indexed lists of original class EntityIds.
  Class creation records these once, including forward declarations. Lowering
  reaches only a function's classes and each class's own written members when
  reproducing the C++ deferred naming order.
- Block scopes produce canonical function-scope ABI nodes. Main and C-linkage
  contexts use the oracle's raw scope node; constructor contexts always use the
  complete entry. Class type and scope names share a per-entity discriminator;
  non-inline contexts consume the existing function/name mangling counter with
  local static objects, while inline outer contexts use declaration ordinals.
  Source LowIR labels and constructor complete/base lifetimes remain distinct.
- Shared-local linkage is separate from discriminator policy. Named class
  ancestry, enclosing inline/member status, internal linkage and parameter
  locality decide internal versus weak binding. A local class in an eligible
  inline function can share a member, while a static inline function or a
  member with local types in its parameter list retains internal linkage.
  Function-owned result types and shared local result types retain their
  frozen exceptions. Local methods and constructor variants carry prefer_local.
- The semantic dump retains enclosing constants as source id expressions and
  renders explicit this with the C++ id-expression pointer spelling. The
  ownership regression checks unchanged AST storage, original declaration/type
  identity, per-function class ownership and the distinction from an ordinary
  parameter read.

Rust owners also include the original declaration/default, scalar
operator/cast/conversion, selected call/operator, lowering fact and symbol
consumers. `docs/port/source-map.tsv` records the exact C++/Rust responsibilities.
Architecture review: classes and expressions keep compact unit-owned IDs;
private counters and indexes hold semantic identities, never rendered names.
Locality queries visit only ownership ancestors and signature types; function
ABI scope and class discriminator results are memoized on their existing owner.
The deferred-name walk is indexed by its outer function and does not inspect
unrelated declarations. Lowering consumes recorded source facts into the same
per-function typed LowIR builder. No second syntax tree, token/text transport,
new optimization, whole-program retry, environment read, process fallback,
dependency, unsafe code or lint suppression was introduced. Optimizer, native
encoder and allocator code are unchanged. No fixture, reference, protected
control, checker policy, oracle source or Ralph state was edited.

## Runtime bit-field accesses and initialization checkpoint

Implementation base HEAD: `1e34321` (full base: `1e34321a5e08ba4997846ff5b7a1867105cdbcb5`).
Stage base and last-audited commit above are unchanged. This is a coherent
partial implementation handoff; **PA11 remains incomplete**. Continuation
classification: **progress**, established by the new production owners and
fixed-manifest reduction below. Ralph owns acceptance, audits and advancement.

### Completed group, direct C++ owners and typed flow

Read the repository guide, contract, specification, conversion plan, PA11
handout and current ledger. Clustered the remaining field cases at the ready
layout-to-storage boundary. Consulted frozen
`lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::bit_field/load_unit/store_unit/read_bit_field/
declared_width/sign_extend/write_bit_field/merge_bit_field/to_unit/
initialize_bit_field/bit_field_value/update_bit_field/resolve_bit_field`,
`structured_class`, `aggregate_class`, `helper_members` and `helper_shape`;
`expression_lowering.cpp`'s operand/place/discard/side-effect rules;
`conversion_lowering.cpp::WideningFolds/convert_integer`;
`object_lowering.cpp::initialize_member/construct_base/subobject_bit_limit`;
`program_lowering.cpp::member_holder`,
`global_lowering.cpp::static_aggregate/flush_bits`, and the enum and symbolic
place owners in `sema/analyzer_classes.cpp`, `constant_expressions.cpp` and
`constant_values.h`.

- `lower/bit_fields.rs` consumes completed width/bit/unit-offset/bytes/shared/
  split facts. It preserves access-type selection, narrowed integer types,
  low-piece-first split assembly/stores through 64/128-bit units, masks,
  unsigned shifts, declared-width copies/extensions/truncation and signed
  extraction. All storage accesses remain untyped and retain volatility.
  Negative values and masks use explicit target bits, checked host conversions
  and target wrapping; no layout is recomputed and no optimization is added.
- `lower/field_updates.rs` preserves assignment address-before-RHS order,
  compound read/RHS/conversion order, prefix readdressing only for the oracle's
  side-effect-free forms, postfix old values, nested updates, comma effects,
  conditional selection and a shared evaluated RHS. Ordinary conditional arms
  retain ordinary object updates. Unsupported designators preserve the frozen
  located diagnostic, including nested conditional designator boundaries.
  A volatile update returns the recorded written value, matching C++.
- Aggregate and constructor initialization preserve source value/address order,
  first whole-unit stores versus merged updates, sparse initializers, prior
  subobject high-water marks and nontrivial base-entry write limits. Recursive
  aggregate state is saved/restored per object, so repeated array elements and
  nested subobjects do not share initialization history. Structured class
  members address before evaluating their value. The frozen helper eligibility
  excludes bit-fields; dynamic local arrays initialize them in place.
- Member accesses consume original member/base routes and accumulate anonymous
  holder offsets from recorded storage edges. Plain implicit members retain
  `this` ownership. Ordinary scalar-place requests retain the oracle's field
  diagnostic instead of inventing a byte address.
- Semantic field storage retains canonical operation TypeIds and represented
  signedness once. Bool and enum stepping types remain their own; narrow
  fundamental fields use width-sensitive promotion. Unfixed enum signedness
  comes from the original enumerator values, independently of the inferred
  underlying type. Symbolic places keep a bit-field EntityId distinct from
  their byte offset. Const-reference snapshots honor the recorded temporary
  conversion, then materialize a scalar in the function's existing storage.
- Static aggregate images skip unnamed fields and accumulate exact named bits
  into bounded units, emitting the C++ unsigned item width and padding order.
  Shared/split units decline static images as the oracle does and retain the
  explicit unfinished dynamic-global boundary.

Rust owners: the two field modules, `lower/aggregates.rs`, `construction.rs`,
`aggregate_helpers.rs`, expression/place/discard/conversion/reference consumers,
`lower/data.rs`, `sema/bit_fields.rs`, `enumerations.rs`, `static_arrays.rs`,
`constant_addresses.rs`, `expression_facts.rs`, `lowering.rs` and the semantic
ownership regression. `docs/port/source-map.tsv` records the responsibilities.
The regression independently verifies original syntax storage, canonical
operation types, bool behavior and enum value signedness.

Architecture review: declaration-owned compact BitFieldIds address the private
unit-owned store; ready layout and expression facts remain separate. Lowering
borrows those facts and emits typed LowIR into one function. Anonymous holder
work visits only its ownership chain; each split unit has at most four pieces.
Initialization state is a fixed-size function-owned record scoped to a
subobject. Static unit scratch is eight initialized bytes. No whole-program
scan/retry, semantic lookup, text transport, second syntax tree, environment
read, process fallback, dependency, unsafe code or lint expectation was added.
Optimizer, native encoding and allocator code are unchanged. Fixtures,
references, protected controls/policy, oracle sources and Ralph state are
unchanged; independent generated probes stay in artifacts.

`400-packed-bit-field-split-access.t` and `400-packed-bit-field-wide-split.t`,
now stop at **dynamic global array initialization**, status 86. Their first
shared owner is `lower/global_lowering.cpp`'s deferred startup actions and
constexpr images, not packed local access. Port namespace initializer demand,
zero/static image choices, startup emission/action order and finalization
before claiming those cases. Copy/move assignment-specific alternate field
initialization order remains with the later value/copy owner. Implicit
anonymous-member receiver resolution remains in its semantic owner.
Other class-prvalue/value transfer, lifecycle/EH, static/TLS, inherited
constructor, local class, literal/operator and declaration groups remain.

## Bit-field declarations and storage-unit layout checkpoint

Implementation base HEAD: `051cbc219f132a134a6eec518fcb370aee4e96f0`.
Stage base and last-audited commit above are unchanged. This is an implementation
checkpoint and a coherent partial assignment handoff; PA11 is not complete.
Continuation classification: **progress**. The preceding checkpoint implemented
class import hiding; the intervening maintainer refresh changed the frozen oracle
and counted control manifest. Fresh entry evidence establishes **263/409**, **146
failures**, before this implementation. The read-only supplied stageProgress log
still compares 240/349 (109 failures) with that refreshed 263/409 result. Its
cross-manifest baseline is a reproducible gate accounting defect: 60 newly
counted cases include 37 failures, while the old 349 identities improve from
109 to 102 failures and retain all 240 old passes. The current 409 identities
improve from 146 to 139 and retain all 263 entry passes. Run
`python3 /home/vishvananda/work/private/v5rust/artifacts/pa11-bit-layout/progress-baseline-reproducer.py`
to compare the retained old checkpoint, fresh entry and final logs; the JSON
receipt retains the exact gains and counts. The old progress log is not changed
or treated as a successful gate. This independent port work proceeds under the
unchanged contract; Ralph owns progress checks, audits and advancement.

### Completed group and direct owners

Read the repository guide, contract, specification, conversion plan, PA11
handout and current ledger. Clustered entry failures by their first divergent
owner; 26 fixed identities stopped at bit-field declarations. Consulted frozen
`sema/analyzer_declarations.cpp::bit_field_declaration/add_data_member`,
`layout.cpp::class_layout/assign_bit_units/assign_bit_segment/assign_bit_fields`,
`analyzer_classes.cpp`'s empty-class rules, `analyzer_expressions.cpp`'s field
promotion/address/reference/sizeof rules, `conversions.cpp::reference`,
`analyzer_initialization.cpp`'s unnamed-field handling, and the downstream
`lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp` and `expression_lowering.cpp` access owners.
The source map records the new responsibilities and the unfinished access boundary.

- Named and unnamed integral/enum fields retain width, declaration identity,
  access and storage ownership. Zero-width unnamed separators are permitted;
  named zero width, negative/scoped/nonconstant width, invalid field type and
  alignment specifiers preserve the frozen diagnostic location and order.
  Declarators are analyzed once and their typed result is reused for ordinary
  declaration settlement. Unnamed physical fields add no indexed name.
- Layout preserves mixed declared-type packing, no natural-unit straddling,
  zero-width separators, wide-field padding/alignment, unnamed-field class
  alignment, union units, GNU packed and pragma pack, and reusable base tails.
  Zero-width-only classes remain empty. Byte offsets of ordinary members are
  separate from bit offsets of fields. POD runs may reach full class size;
  non-POD runs stop at member data size so derived members can reuse the tail.
- Each run records exact bounded storage windows, largest natural-object
  segments, narrowed units, overlap sharing, and packed split units up to 16
  bytes. Target unsigned arithmetic wraps explicitly; published host-size and
  compact-ID conversions are checked. This checkpoint does not substitute a
  simpler field-by-field allocation scheme.
- Original expressions retain `GlvalueKind` and optional direct `BitFieldId`
  designation separately. Prefix, assignment, comma and conditional glvalues
  propagate the flag without inventing a designation; field-width promotion
  affects ordinary arithmetic and conversion rank. Address, sizeof and selected
  local/return reference binding checks follow the frozen owner. A qualified
  member named without an object in sizeof retains the oracle's type-only path.
  Call argument applications retain the frozen semantic behavior; the unfinished
  lowering owner supplies their later bit-field-place diagnostic.
- Aggregate initialization skips unnamed fields, preserving initializer
  consumption and union selection. A required runtime bit-field access reaches
  explicit status 86 at the new typed storage boundary; it never treats the
  bit offset as an ordinary byte offset or silently emits a scalar access.

Rust owners: `sema/bit_fields.rs`, `bit_layout.rs`, `layout.rs`, `graph.rs`,
`declarations.rs`, `expression_facts.rs`, member/scalar expression/conversion
owners, `queries.rs`, `aggregate_initialization.rs`, `layout_attributes.rs`,
`lowering.rs`, `lower/aggregates.rs` and the semantic ownership regression.
The private field store lives with Graph; Analyzer owns reusable run/segment
scratch, clears contents and restores capacity on successful and failing layout
paths. Segment formation and assignment visit each field a bounded number of
times. Class layout is memoized and demanded only by its existing consumers;
there is no unrelated class/body scan, reparsing, second semantic tree, name
recovery or broader backend lifetime. Optimizer/native code is unchanged.
No unsafe code, compiler fallback, dependency or lint suppression was added.

Two new required regressions are in the executed PA11 `.t` control lane:
`bit-field-layout-boundaries.t` and
`bit-field-width-before-later-alignment-bad.t`. The first draft used `.cpp`,
which the refreshed course lane did not execute; these new files were moved to
`.t` before the final course validation. Frozen fixtures, references, protected
checks/policy and Ralph state are unchanged. A semantic unit regression checks
unchanged original syntax storage, an undemanded class, shared natural units,
a narrowed member run, a non-POD tail and 9-byte packed split storage metadata.

### Resource measurements and protected stack qualification

`measure.py`, `measure.log`, `resources.json`, `scaling.jsonl` and
`binary-hashes.json` retain A/A calibration and paired ABBA observations on
100/400/1,600 ordinary query functions and 400/1,600/6,400 classes respectively.
The supported generated inputs exercise mixed natural units, narrowed runs,
non-POD tails and derived layout; every protected comparison and all measured
LowIR output hashes match. Observations include startup on this shared host,
were collected after validation processes completed, and are not a performance
or generated-code optimization gate.

| Query functions / classes | Rust mean ms | Rust peak RSS KiB | C++ mean ms | C++ peak RSS KiB |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 100 / 400 | 35.68 | 8744 | 11.46 | 9752 |
| 400 / 1600 | 69.84 | 14396 | 29.69 | 13728 |
| 1600 / 6400 | 212.15 | 45396 | 88.36 | 32732 |

The 512-level field-width expression starts both tools at a 1 MiB stack using
the protected adapter. `stack.jsonl` records **match=true** and
`stack_qualification.passed=true` at 16 MiB, both exit 0 with identical outputs.
Raw initial/retry attempts remain in the receipt's `stack-qualifications` path.
The receipt, not the original returned exhaustion streams or physical cutoff,
is the proof. The genuine byte-budget guard is unchanged.

### Next dependency boundary

Bit-field declarations, semantic designation and exact storage-unit layout are
complete at this boundary. The next coherent group is runtime bit-field
lowering, owned by `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp::bit_field/load_unit/store_unit/
read_bit_field/write_bit_field/merge_bit_field/initialize_bit_field/update_bit_field`
and its expression/object-lowering callers. Consume the recorded unit metadata
without recalculating layout: preserve access-type selection, signed/enum
extraction, volatile reads, masks, shared-unit merging, split-piece order,
assignment results, prefix/postfix updates, conditional/comma target selection,
constructor and aggregate initialization and repeated-subobject state. The 19
remaining fixed bit-field/access identities now meet that boundary. Other class
value/temporary, lifecycle/EH, static/global/TLS, inherited constructor, local
class, literal/operator and declaration groups remain in the exact residual set.

## Class using-declaration identity and signature hiding checkpoint

Implementation base HEAD: `25e553f4785ac21288409af22dc9df5918c6e050`.
The stage base and last-audited commit above are unchanged. This is an
**implementation** checkpoint, not a stage advancement or independent audit.
Continuation classification: **progress**. The preceding turn certified its
reviewed implementation and validation in the ledger; this entry revalidated
the clean checkout and unchanged **238/349**, **111 failures**, then translated
the next shared declaration/import owner. Ralph retains gate/audit ownership.

### Completed group and direct C++ owners

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, the entire PA11 handout,
`docs/rust-clone-plan.md` and the current ledger. Consulted the frozen C++
`sema/analyzer.cpp::using_declaration/introduce`,
`analyzer_declarations.cpp::earlier_function/new_function/check_member_overload`,
`analyzer_access.cpp::check_access`, `lookup.cpp::remove_function/declare` and
`semantic_unit.h` directly. The three fixed using-signature failures shared
those declaration owners. Adjacent ADL/callable-field cases already reach
class copy, value initialization or subobject destruction; they are not
misclassified as declaration-import defects.

- A class's own function hides an imported base function with the same
  canonical parameter list, variadic flag, cv and ref qualifiers, regardless
  of return type and declaration order. An earlier own declaration suppresses
  the import; a later one removes the import from the class's name sets and
  creates a distinct own entity. Namespace/block conflicts retain the frozen
  diagnostics. Ordinary and operator names follow the same typed rule.
- Every imported function's access is checked before any hidden signature is
  discarded, including private overloads otherwise hidden by an own function.
  The C++ using-declaration check also runs in type-dump mode. Protected imports
  retain the declaration's access and do not invent an acted-on object.
- Class redeclaration and static/ref overload restrictions inspect only own
  functions. A using import does not turn a new derived member into a base
  redeclaration or impose restrictions that apply only to one class's members.
  Out-of-class qualified declarations/definitions require a matching own
  function. A class import yields the qualified-definition mismatch; a
  namespace import reaches the original using-conflict diagnostic. A final
  review probe found the class-owner filter initially intercepted namespace
  conflicts; its restriction to class scopes and a structured-error regression
  now preserve both diagnostic boundaries.
- Removal preserves canonical base entity/origin identity, all source-facing
  declarations, other scopes' indexes and surviving overload order. Repeated
  imports update the surviving indexed binding without duplicate entities.
  The private removal API repairs both declared/visible positions and the
  ordinary/special-name representative. The ownership regression checks
  multiple removals, repeated imports and original AST expression counts.

Rust owners are `sema/declarations.rs`, `function_names.rs`, `graph.rs` and
`tests.rs`; the source map records each translated responsibility. Queries
use the existing ordinal cursor rather than allocating copied candidate
vectors. Removal touches only the selected `(ScopeId, ValueKey)` set and its
shifted `EntityId` positions; its cost is proportional to that set's suffix,
with no global invalidation or unrelated declaration/body scan. `BindingId`
source records remain alive with their unit owner. Original call facts retain
selected function IDs and conversions, so lowering consumes the chosen own
member or surviving base import without relookup, reparsing or extra demand.
The optimizer, code generator and per-function backend lifetimes are unchanged.
No unsafe code, fallback, new dependency or lint suppression was introduced.

Four new regressions are in PA11's executed control lane:
`class-using-signature-hiding.cpp`, `class-using-hidden-private-import-bad.cpp`,
`class-using-import-ref-qualifiers.cpp`, `class-using-import-definition-bad.cpp`.
Existing frozen inputs/references, policy/checker files and Ralph state are
unchanged. The first control draft admitted an ambiguous inherited operator
call in both tools; its base call now uses an explicit base qualifier. This
was a new-probe expectation correction, not an oracle/reference change.

## Independent audit of base construction, friends and standard operators

Continuation classification: **progress**. Entry was a clean `ea2058d` checkout.
The accepted checkpoint and fresh protected entry gate both have **238/349**
passing cases, **111 failures**. This review certifies only the groups marked
complete in the three checkpoint records below. It does not certify full PA11
or require later unimplemented behavior to pass. The stage base is unchanged.
Reviewed implementation commit including fixes:
`6122c4e960ce8f5204de24fc16f976caf0818dab`. The last-audited pointer above
now names that exact implementation. The following certification changes only
this ledger; validated compiler/fixture/source-map content remains identical.

### Findings and fixes

1. **Array/function reference operator results were loaded before decay.**
   `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::rvalue/glvalue/operator_call` returns the
   selected call's address directly for array/function results. Rust's new
   operator fast path ran before its ordinary array/function decay path and
   emitted a spurious `load obj<12x4>` or `load ptr`. The fix reads a returned
   place only for value-bearing referents; arrays/functions preserve the call
   address. No new semantic tree or conversion selection is introduced.
   The original raw diffs and input identities are in `finding-evidence.json`.
   `tests/controls/operator-reference-decay.cpp` exercises direct array indexing,
   function calls and pointer initialization and passes raw comparison plus
   native course execution.
2. **Protected member functions omitted the acted-on-object check.**
   `analyzer_access.cpp::NonStaticMember/check_access/check_function_access`
   applies 11.4 to nonstatic functions as well as fields. Rust's shared access
   validator only tested instance data members, admitting a protected operator
   on a Base object from a derived member or friend. It now also tests the
   selected nonstatic function identity, preserving static-member access and
   the using-declaration's recorded access. The exact oracle rejection position
   and bytes match. `protected-operator-receiver-bad.cpp` and
   `protected-callable-friend-receiver-bad.cpp` are executed PA11 regressions;
   additional probes cover explicit method calls, correct derived receivers,
   direct friends and protected static methods.
3. **Out-of-class members of friend classes lost signature access.**
   `analyzer_access.cpp::member_declaration_class/member_or_friend_of` and
   `analyzer_declarations.cpp::simple_declaration/function_definition` retain
   the lexical member-class context for leading declaration specifiers.
   Rust's settlement recognized a selected function's own friendship or direct
   declaring class, but not that class's friendship or enclosing classes.
   The repaired typed context traverses only those lexical classes and their
   indexed grants. Leading specifier analysis temporarily uses the qualified
   member's class, then restores it on every Result path. Signature settlement
   retains the chosen function's lexical class chain and restores its function
   context. No rendered signature or second parser is used.
   `friend-class-member-signature.cpp` checks a private result/parameter/default,
   nested declarations and an out-of-class definition; the rejecting
   `friend-class-signature-context-bad.cpp` prevents access leaking to the next
   unrelated function. Static data definitions and nested-member definitions
   are separately verified in type/semantic views; their still-unimplemented
   ABI lowering is not claimed complete.
4. **Operator and ADL work allocated fresh containers per query.**
   Standard operator lookup created a new candidate vector/entity set, while
   association created type/class/namespace work vectors and visited maps/sets
   for every use. They now belong to reusable typed query scratch on Analyzer;
   each query takes its scratch, clears its contents and restores capacity on
   success or failure. Lookup still visits the indexed member, lexical ordinary
   and associated sets in the original order. Enum builtin parameters and
   lowered operator argument values use inline pairs; nested calls keep their
   operand values local. The new unit regression runs an ambiguous operator
   query followed by a successful query, checks empty restored scratch and
   retained capacity, and proves unchanged original expression storage.

These defects are fixed independently of fixture counts. The original fixed
manifest is unchanged; new required regressions are in PA11's executed control
lane. No frozen source, reference, checker, gate policy or Ralph state was
modified. A first local test compile had an incorrect import; it was corrected
before the final strict quality run, without suppression.

| `lower/expression_lowering.cpp::operator_call/glvalue/discard`, `condition_lowering.cpp` | `lower/operator_calls.rs`, `expressions.rs`, `mutations.rs`, `conditions.rs`: original inline operand view; object address before left-to-right arguments; postfix target zero; returned reference addresses, discarded calls without referent reads, overloaded logical calls stopping builtin short-circuit spines |
| `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp::function_encoding` | `lower/symbols.rs`: count the nonstatic implicit object when selecting unary/binary Itanium operator terminals; retain sanitized LowIR symbol collision and first-demand order |

This group covers ordinary non-template operators whose viable arguments use
existing standard/reference/list conversions: unary, prefix/postfix, binary,
comparison, logical, comma, written assignment/compound assignment, member
subscript, hidden/namespace functions and callable class objects. It includes
chained reference-returning operators, inherited member object projections,
nonmember/member cv competition, builtin fallback when no function is viable,
ambiguity and deleted/private best candidates, enum-only overload eligibility
and same-enum builtin comparison competition. Postfix dummy zero is emitted
without synthesizing an expression node or adding it to the ranking operands.

### Typed data flow, lifetimes and architecture review

`ValueKey::Special(Operator)` owns lookup identity independently of spelling.
All candidates and conversions use BindingId/EntityId, ScopeId, ExprId and
canonical TypeId identities. Selected calls live on the original expression;
parentheses share their existing dense fact record. CallForm distinguishes
operator expansion from ordinary calls without a second semantic tree.
The ownership regression checks unchanged syntax storage, selected entity
identity through a reference-returning chain, original class lvalues and
recorded reference/integer argument sequences.

`OperatorScratch` owns query candidates and aligned sequences, reusing capacity
and clearing content on every Result path. Lookup visits only the left class's
indexed members, its lexical ordinary lookup and argument-associated indexes;
no unrelated class registry, text signature, whole-program retry or invalidation
was added. Enum builtin candidates use compact optional callable identity.
The original iterative binary spine stays bounded and restores its mark.
Class-call resolution uses the existing reusable call scratch after nested
arguments finish. Default arguments and exception specifications retain their
previous declaration owners and demand boundaries.

Lowering consumes selected entity and conversion/path facts directly. Its
one/two-operand view uses inline IDs; argument values and frame/storage scratch
belong to the current function. Reference-result places use the returned pointer;
discarding one emits its effects without loading the referent. An overloaded
logical operator evaluates both arguments, while builtin logical expressions
retain their short circuit and block ordering. ABI terminal arity includes the
implicit object without changing operator lookup or emitted parameter lists.
Unused operator definitions retain semantic checking and first-demand emission.
No optimizer, register allocator or native encoder change was made.

No unsafe, FFI, subprocess implementation, environment reads in compiler phases,
new dependency, blanket lint suppression, fixture recognition, protected input,
checker/oracle policy or Ralph state modification was introduced. Original
fixture coverage is unchanged. Independent probes are generated outside the
checkout; the added Rust unit test protects production ownership invariants.

The first earlier-stage validation caught a real PA6 course regression:
`pa6/tests/general/875-function-name-operator-decltype-id.t` must reject the
operator name in types mode. The C++ `lookup_name` owner still accepts only
identifiers; semantics has its separate operator-function lookup. The Rust
lookup now preserves that distinction. `prior-development.log` retains the
finding; final validation below uses the corrected binary.

The frozen qualified-friend owner matches a previously declared parameter
signature; it does not compare that friend's written return/exception spelling
again. The port preserves that observable behavior, verified by independent
return/noexcept probes. Unqualified redeclarations still perform the normal
return/exception agreement checks. A qualified friend contributes access only,
not an extra declaration, definition, default argument or hidden ADL edge.

The group covers hidden declarations and definitions, overloads and repeated
friend declarations, later visibility, unnamed namespaces, private field access,
private signature types/defaults, friendship through derived bases, hidden tag
revelation and nearest-scope friend class lookup. The five new executed PA11
controls are `friendship-identity-and-access.cpp`,
`friend-signature-overload-access-bad.cpp`,
`friend-class-namespace-boundary-bad.cpp`,
`hidden-friend-ordinary-lookup-bad.cpp` and
`hidden-friend-preserves-visible-type.cpp`. The positive access control also
calls both hidden overloads and exercises enclosing-class ADL for a nested type
and enum. Controls supplement the frozen manifest; they do not count as gains.

### Typed data flow, lifetimes and architecture review

The original parser tree is unchanged during analysis. `EntityId`, `BindingId`,
`ScopeId`, `TypeId` and typed ValueKey identity own every friendship, candidate,
signature, access check and symbol input. Function selection still records its
chosen declaration and conversions on the original expression facts. Lowering
consumes those facts; it performs no new lookup or resolution.

`Friendships` owns sparse forward/reverse grants, hidden binding facts and
class/name ADL edges for one translation unit. The graph owns separate declared
and visible candidate vectors with indexed entity positions. Declaration
matching probes a namespace/name/canonical-signature key; visible namespace ADL
never scans unrelated hidden friends. Immutable canonical signature memos live
with Types; declaration indexes are updated only for their own binding key, and
visible source ordinals change only when the first visible declaration arrives.
No global invalidation, unrelated retry, whole-program polling or rendered
signature key was added.

Declaration access has a separate transient owner. Pending records contain
already resolved typed IDs; settlement applies access in the declared function's
friend/member context and never reparses or retypes syntax. Optional object
identity distinguishes naming a member from access through a particular object,
preserving implicit protected access. Nested declaration marks restore the
context and truncate scratch on success or error. Friend bodies remain analyzed
as the C++ owner requires even when they are unused, and are emitted only on
existing demand. No fabricated body, second semantic tree or frontend identity
crosses the typed LowIR boundary.

The Rust ownership regression checks unchanged expression storage, one canonical
namespace function shared by two granting classes, deduplicated friendship
edges and a hidden ADL binding still usable before the later namespace visibility
point. The existing overload-visibility unit test now constructs actual function
types instead of scalar placeholders so it exercises the stronger typed index
invariant; its original interleaved-visibility assertions are retained.

Per-function LowIR/backend storage, the optimizer, register allocator and native
code generator retain their existing lifetimes and behavior. No unsafe code,
FFI, subprocess implementation, compiler-environment read, dependency, fixture
recognition, protected policy change or new optimization was introduced.

| `sema/semantic_unit.h` | `sema/construction.rs`, `lowering.rs`: retained `BaseInitialization`, compact `ConstructorId`, memoized `Triviality`, borrowed lowering views and checked ready layout spans |
| `lower/object_lowering.cpp` (`construct_base`, `construct`, `call_special`, `tail_occupied`) | `lower/construction.rs`, `aggregates.rs`: consume base facts; address-before-argument, base-before-member order; C2 calls; original defaults/reference conversions; aggregate base lists; empty-base value suppression and occupied-tail data-only zeroing |
| `lower/symbol_lowering.cpp`, `program_lowering.cpp` | `lower/symbols.rs`, `function.rs`, `mod.rs`: distinct external C2 declarations and definition metadata; defer typed C2 aliases until all input units finish; omit aliases when C2 has an independent definition |
| `lower/definition_order.cpp` | `lower/definition_order.rs`, `symbols.rs`, `driver/ast.rs`: base-before-complete family rank within each family's original completion positions; external ABI-name identity and per-unit local family identity |

Supported bases with trivial destruction now run written direct/list/value
initialization or omitted default construction, including defaulted/implicit
transitive chains, scalar/reference/default/ellipsis arguments and supported
aggregate base lists. A mem-initializer hidden by a static member still names
that member and rejects; duplicate aliases name one base identity. Ordinary
complete base construction inside a derived member receives the protected-object
rejection, while constructing its actual base is accessible. Inaccessible/deleted
base destructors contribute the inherited constructor's deletion or the written
constructor's use checks before body analysis.

Selection reuses the indexed constructor candidate owner and records conversions
on original ExprIds once. Original initializers/argument shapes remain on the
base actions, separate from callable/body plans. Default plans are completed
bottom-up and memoize full transitive triviality; use sites do not walk all base
members to recover it. Layout demand remains explicit. Lowering uses retained
class/edge identities and ready offsets, never member lookup or another overload
query. Complete/base zeroing differs where a base entry's nonvirtual write limit
occupies a child's tail; empty bases write no value-initialization byte.

Definition ordering retains one serialized byte buffer plus spans and compact
program-owned family IDs. It permutes only a constructor family's occupied
positions, preserving every intervening unrelated definition. External families
use typed ABI NameIds; local IDs are allocated per constructor in its unit.
There is no text parsing, ABI-name grammar reconstruction, duplicate owning AST,
retained function body, or frontend identity crossing the unit boundary. Pending
aliases contain only LowIR object/symbol IDs and are decided at program finish.
Each function's LowIR/backend scratch is still released after streaming.
Optimizer, register allocator, native backend and protected policies are unchanged.

The new executed PA11 control is `tests/controls/base-constructor-actions.cpp`:
reversed written initializer order, an aliased protected base, reference/default
arguments, repeated C1/C2 demand, a three-level implicit chain, and value zeroing.
The new semantic ownership test verifies unchanged syntax size, shared ready base
plans and one retained demand for repeated objects, including transitive
nontriviality. The existing base-plan identity test now checks the richer typed
base records rather than a vector of bare constructor IDs.

## Independent audit of the three accepted partial checkpoints

Reviewed implementation commit: `b6d3b46241f7e221ead0dc004e5909a9d747afe6`.
This audit certifies the completed partial groups, not PA11 stage completion.
The earlier checkpoint sections below are historical records; this section
supersedes their audit pointer and validation claims. Ralph retains stage and
phase ownership; its state and existing goal were not edited or replaced.

Continuation classification: **progress**. The preceding implementation turn
committed the nonvirtual-layout group at `7295240`; this audit inspected the clean
actual checkout, read the required guide/contract/spec/plan/handout/ledger and
source map, reran the entry gate and reviewed the entire accepted implementation
diff since `6cdff3d` against the frozen C++ owners. Five in-scope findings were
fixed, four executed regressions added, and fresh evidence collected. Fixture
counts alone are not the audit argument.

### Findings and fixes

1. **Alignment operands were not typed before constant evaluation.**
   `alignas(&object)` incorrectly reported `operator is not allowed in a constant
   expression` in semantic/LowIR modes. Frozen
   `analyzer_classes.cpp::attribute_alignment` uses
   `constant_evaluator.cpp::constant_operand` before evaluation. The Rust owner
   now records/resolves original operand facts once in semantic modes, so its
   existing integral evaluator reports the exact `not an integral constant
   expression` bytes and position. Type-only mode retains its original evaluator.
   Unknown names, nonconstant objects, undefined calls and unresolved overload
   addresses also match; no alignment value is inferred from syntax spelling.
2. **Related ordinary-pointer composites omitted derivation.**
   `condition ? derived_pointer : base_pointer` and comparisons rejected valid
   uses. Frozen `analyzer_member_pointers.cpp::composite_pointer` selects the base
   pointee and unions both cv sets. `sema/scalar_operators.rs` now implements
   that shared branch with canonical TypeIds and the existing hierarchy query.
   The existing conversion owner records the retained path, ambiguity/access
   checks and layout demand; lowering consumes those facts. Both operand orders,
   nulls, cv unions, unrelated pointers, private and ambiguous bases were checked.
   Nonzero multiple-base scratch probes check the shared algorithm without
   claiming the later multiple-inheritance assignment complete.
3. **Comparison projection order was wrong.** After selecting the composite,
   Rust evaluated the right operand before adjusting the left base pointer.
   Frozen `expression_lowering.cpp::comparison` performs that adjustment first.
   `lower/expressions.rs` now consumes the recorded left path before evaluating
   the right operand, including a zero-offset projection. Other scalar conversion
   ordering remains intact. Raw LowIR checks cover null-test blocks, temporary
   numbering, zero/nonzero offsets and effectful right operands.

4. **Pointer reference temporaries lost base adjustments.** The residual
   `tests/spec/200-const-reference-binds-derived-pointer-prvalue.t` passed a
   derived pointer through a `Base * const&` temporary without its byte index.
   Frozen `bind_converted` / `convert_implicit` converts before storing that
   temporary. `lower/references.rs` now consumes the retained offset and preserves
   null even for a written address expression, as that C++ implicit conversion
   requires; Cast context retains its separate rule. Ordinary calls, local
   reference storage, constructor arguments and conditional pointer inputs match
   at zero and nonzero offsets. No conversion selection occurs in lowering.
5. **An implicit copy/move candidate set was treated as empty.** For a single
   non-converting initialization such as `struct X { int m=1; }; X x{2};`,
   `constructor_candidates` offers implicit copy/move constructors. None can
   take the scalar, so C++ reports `no viable function for the call`; Rust
   incorrectly said `no constructor for the initialization`. The existing
   typed copy selector now checks that offered set when no declared constructor
   is offered, using standard sequences, canonical identities and restored
   scratch. No copy body is demanded. Successful class-list copy initialization
   and implicit copy ranking from nested braced arguments retain explicit
   status-86 boundaries rather than being silently rejected or implemented here.

and protected raw comparison. They execute conditional selection, qualification

rejection. These fixes also add three fixed-manifest successes:

### Completed behavior and architecture review

The existing iterative layout dependency worklist now visits bases before members
and reads only ready child layouts. Each class retains complete size/alignment,
nonvirtual data size, emptiness, empty-subobject containment and volatile/union
containment. POD bases retain tail padding; non-POD bases expose their unpadded
nonvirtual size. Derived classes are non-POD for layout. Direct base edges retain
explicit optional byte offsets. GNU packed leaves bases alone; pragma pack caps
nonempty bases, while empty bases keep their natural alignment. Class alignment
strengthens the final size after base/member placement, including unused classes
whose nonzero alignment request already demands layout.

`EmptyPlacement` belongs to one class-placement query. Its typed `(EntityId,
byte offset)` set and explicit stack detect empty subobjects of equal type and
address in bases, ordinary members and array elements. Empty bases start at zero;
collisions move by natural alignment, and ordinary member collisions preserve the
C++ rounding rule. Conflict hints, reachable-record limits and array gap skipping
match the C++ owner. Billion-element arrays do not create per-element facts or
walk elements wholly inside an unreachable gap. The exact 2^20 semantic work
budget is retained. No native recursion, unrelated global scan or output-sensitive
hash iteration was added. Existing active/ready layout states retain cycle and
incomplete-class errors and demand-only publication.

Semantic analysis retains interned paths on the original expression: mutable use
conversions, member-holder/naming routes and the original explicit cast's route
are distinct. The last has recorded direction and zero-projection policy, so an
outer conversion cannot overwrite a cast's original path. Unit assertions verify
that distinction with a cast from one base followed by conversion to another,
along with unused layout demand, data-size and empty-identity ownership. No
syntax node is copied or synthesized for analysis. Lowering mechanically sums
ready edge offsets; a missing path/layout remains an invariant error.

Inherited field and method receivers emit the C++ byte index even at offset zero.
An intermediate qualified naming class preserves two separate projections.
Reference binding and reference casts project the designated subobject; downcasts
negate the recorded target offset with explicit wrapping and signed i64 literal
presentation. Pointer conversions retain null through the C++ `basecast` result
slot and null/adjust/end blocks when the offset is nonzero. Only `this` and an
ordinary address-of expression suppress that null test. First designation and
selected-overload subobject checks preserve ambiguity/access precedence. A public
using declaration over a private base admits its selected member through that
declaration; physical receiver adjustment does not recheck access to the whole
base. Explicit class-to-naming conversions still check access.

The semantic view prints original implicit-this field receivers and explicit/
implicit call object arguments, including the selected base-pointer cast wrapper.
Demanded bodies use the recorded canonical origin binding rather than a using
alias. The printer traverses retained facts and source IDs without lookup,
retyping or demand. Backend, optimizer and register allocator code is unchanged;
all emitted body/slot/block scratch still belongs to one function.

Multi-unit validation found and fixed the adjacent inline emission boundary.
Already defined inline entries are skipped before constructing body scratch or
streaming, preserving strong-definition rejection and preventing a spurious
declaration of the previously streamed function. Functions whose signatures
name unit-local class/enum types get internal symbol ownership; a separate
`DeclaredLinkage` enum retains the declaration's ABI L-marker rule. In particular
a function naming an unnamed-namespace class is unit-local without a static
name. A unit assertion verifies shared inline coalescing, distinct internal
method symbols, retained object-byte boundaries, released bodies and absence of
spurious declarations after both frontends have been released.

Two new executed PA11 controls cover POD/non-POD tails, empty nested arrays,
alignment and packing, inherited field/receiver and pointer/reference/cast use,
and public using declarations over private bases. Both execute successfully and
match protected raw LowIR, type and semantic outputs. Broader scratch probes test
nonzero offsets and multiple nonvirtual paths without claiming PA22 completion.
Bit-fields, overlapping members, virtual layout, anonymous-object projection,
class-prvalue materialization and nontrivial base lifecycle remain separate.

### Completed behavior and architecture review

Original attribute lists retain form, site, expression and type syntax IDs.
Attribute analysis evaluates operands once in their declaration context, folds
the strictest request, and records compact `StorageAttributes` on the canonical
entity. The request's explicit low-32-bit conversion matches the frozen target
record, including zero and truncated requests. Standard and GNU placement rules
remain distinct. Standard alignment on typedefs/functions in simple declarations,
attributes applied to types, weak object/class requests, invalid powers of two,
incomplete type operands and alignas on bit-fields retain exact diagnostics.
Function definitions preserve the frozen exception to simple-declaration alignas
rejection and its attribute-before-body diagnostic order.

Class definitions retain the parser's packing snapshot and prior forward
alignment. A nonzero class request demands layout at completion, including an
unused under-aligned class; unrelated unused classes and member bodies stay lazy.
The existing iterative dependency walk and active/ready memo states remain the
layout owner. Ordinary members use pragma pack's alignment cap; GNU packed uses
one-byte alignment unless the member requests one, and leaves a non-POD class
member unpacked unless that child is itself packed. `LayoutCategory` is an enum,
recorded from declaration facts and propagated with ready child layouts. Private/
protected fields, reference members, DMIs and user-provided special members affect
it; a variadic operator= is excluded from copy-assignment classification exactly
as in C++. Arrays use their element's ready category and effective packed fact.
Class-level alignment strengthens the final size/alignment after member placement.
No string keys, AST copies or recursive dependency traversal are introduced.

Lowering consumes the ready request and layout. Automatic non-reference objects
get an aligned object slot rounded to the stricter alignment, with initialization
and access still using their original semantic types. Local references retain
pointer slots. Namespace and block-static/TLS objects retain their original
scalar/address/zero initializer as structured data when alignment requires it;
the typed span is rounded while zero items retain their logical byte counts.
Bodies, slots, temporaries and LowIR still belong to one function and are released
after streaming. Optimizer/register allocator/native backend code is unchanged.

The new aligned-storage control exposed an existing block-static ABI spelling
mismatch in `main`. Following the C++ owner, the typed encoding for `main` and
C-linkage functions is their bare ABI name; a local scope consumes that node
instead of adding parameter types. Ordinary and namespaced functions keep their
normal encoding. No mangled-name reconstruction or test-specific path is used.

Two executed PA11 controls cover requested-alignment/packing precedence, packed
non-POD arrays, the variadic assignment distinction, aligned scalar/array/
object storage and main's block-static identity. Their course execution
and protected raw output both pass. Existing compiler-input files were not
formatted or edited; missing status 86 remains a failure.

### Behavior and architecture review

Original declarations retain a selected compact `ConstructorId` and typed
`ConstructorArguments` (`Values` borrows the original AST list, `Single` an
original ExprId, `Value` value-initialization). Constructor bodies remain separate
unit-owned plans. List facts retain the selected constructor independently of
argument conversions. Same-class prvalue copying reuses the checked elision path,
with no repeated operand typing or copy-body demand.

Direct, converting and copy-list candidate sets borrow only the owning class's
constructor bindings. The ordinary resolver now returns a compact selection over
reusable scratch, then publishes its recorded argument sequences separately.
Converting initialization filters explicit constructors; copy-list initialization
ranks them and diagnoses a selected explicit constructor before deletedness.
Deletion and access precede argument application; constructor demand follows it.
Defaults, reference bindings, function designations, non-first-argument ranking,
ellipsis promotions, ambiguity and list narrowing share the existing algorithms.
A converting initialization checks final copy/move usability without body demand,
including the frozen converted-temporary selection diagnostic. Scratch clears on
success/error paths; nested argument queries finish before ranking scratch is held.
No per-initialization persistent overload set or synthetic AST is created.

Lowering consumes the selected use, computes the local object's address before
argument effects, binds/fits supplied and default arguments, and uses the existing
complete/base ABI identities and first-demand emission queue. Value initialization
zeroes before a required nontrivial call. Layout completion records volatile/union
containment from typed array elements and child layouts; that ready fact selects
one `zeroinit` span or exact widest-fitting byte stores, with no graph rescan.
Every function still owns and releases its slots, blocks, temporaries and LowIR.
Constructor list throw queries follow selected specification/default-argument
edges and destructor facts. Semantic views print original supplied arguments;
a demanded bodyless defaulted special member prints as its declaration instead
of entering the ordinary-function definition reader.

Three new PA11 executed controls cover selected overloaded/function/reference/
default/braced arguments, volatile/union value-initialization bytes, and
copy-list explicit-before-deleted rejection. Existing compiler inputs were not
formatted or changed. Status 86 remains an incomplete implementation, never an
accepted rejection.

### Next dependency boundary

Port class-prvalue materialization and recursive class/array constructor actions,
then local/subobject destruction and lexical/EH lifetime cleanup. Initialization
uses now reach the existing ABI entry pipeline with selected arguments; recursive
member lowering must consume those facts rather than redo selection. Namespace/
block-static/TLS class initialization and functional class prvalues still need
their own storage/lifetime owners. General user conversions, class-valued arguments,
nonelided copies, initializer-list constructor preference and inherited constructor
plans are not claimed by this scalar/reference local-call group.

Other residual clusters: **25** bit-field declarations, **22** extended declaration
specifiers, **18** base layouts, **11** written base constructor plans, **8** class
storage destruction lowerings, **7** local ABI scopes, **7** extended layouts and
**5** class-subobject default constructions; **56** raw diagnostic/artifact
refinements. `stage-summary.json` retains every exact cluster identity and trace.
Further due PA11 work remains operators/ADL/friends, inheritance/access/layout,
anonymous injection, symbols, global/TLS/static lifetimes, pseudo-destructors,
packing/alignment and cleanup sharing. Later copy/value, template and virtual
features retain the handout's later-stage boundaries.

## Independent checkpoint audit

Read `AGENTS.md`, `CLONE_CONTRACT.md`, `spec.md`, `docs/rust-clone-plan.md`,
the PA11 handout, this ledger and its two preceding checkpoint versions.
Compared the entire accepted diff from the stage base with the frozen C++
commit `ee3596b2df2dadf70d7d2028a6a32d18e1923761` under
`/home/vishvananda/work/references/v5rust-cpp`. The source-map entries retain
responsibility ownership and explicit unfinished boundaries.

Reviewed C++ owners: `sema/analyzer_initialization.cpp`,
`sema/analyzer_construction.cpp`, `sema/analyzer_expressions.cpp`,
`sema/analyzer_calls.cpp`, `sema/analyzer_exceptions.cpp`,
`sema/analyzer_declarations.cpp`, `sema/analyzer_classes.cpp`, `sema/layout.cpp`
and the semantic headers; `lower/aggregate_lowering.cpp`,
`lower/object_lowering.cpp`, `lower/expression_lowering.cpp`,
`lower/discard_lowering.cpp`, `lower/statement_lowering.cpp`,
`lower/function_lowering.cpp`, `lower/global_lowering.cpp`,
`lower/program_lowering.cpp`, `lower/definition_order.cpp`,
`lower/symbol_lowering.cpp` and `lowir/text_writer.cpp`.
Their Rust owners are the mapped `sema/construction.rs`, `initialization.rs`,
`member_expressions.rs`, `scalar_expressions.rs`, `scalar_operators.rs`,
`scalar_conversions.rs`, `procedural_calls.rs`, `function_resolution.rs`,
`special_members.rs`, `throw_queries.rs`, `layout.rs`, `expression_facts.rs`,
`graph.rs`, `lowering.rs`; `lower/aggregate_helpers.rs`, `aggregates.rs`,
`arrays.rs`, `construction.rs`, `conditions.rs`, `expressions.rs`,
`mutations.rs`, `function.rs`, `local_storage.rs`, `globals.rs`, `symbols.rs`,
`mod.rs`, `lowir/writer.rs` and `driver/ast.rs`.

Six findings were fixed in `6cdff3d`:

1. **Static receiver effects and bytes.** Static calls always loaded a pointer
   or computed an object address, including pure receivers. The frozen
   `side_effect_free` / `member_object` query skips pure names, member paths
   and subscripts, distinguishes an address from a volatile value read, and
   otherwise discards the original expression. A shared lowering query now
   serves static calls, data and folded-member accesses. The prior receiver
   ledger's claim that static calls always evaluate an explicit object address
   was incorrect and is superseded here. Effectful receivers still run before
   argument effects; the frozen 64-level semantic query cap is preserved.
2. **String-member helper eligibility.** Bare character-array string clauses
   incorrectly went through aggregate-array helpers, creating temporary slots
   and `copyobj` transfers. The frozen `helper_members` declines a non-list
   expression that directly initializes an array subaggregate. The corrected
   query selects final-member stores for bare strings; braced strings retain
   their helper form. Character widths and element stores use the shared typed
   character/array algorithms.
3. **Static function arguments.** A lone static member used as a call argument
   reached data-member lowering and exited 70 with `member without designation`.
   A read-only semantic API now exposes the recorded selected function identity
   to place lowering, preserving receiver effects, demand and function-address
   order. Separately, bound overload sets now fail candidate viability before
   target selection, matching the frozen `implicit_sequence` rule and exact
   `no viable function for the call` diagnostic. This preserves the oracle's
   rejection even for a bound overload set consisting of static functions.
4. **Defaulted trivial member dependencies.** An explicitly defaulted
   constructor could request its member facts before a trivial class/array
   member had a ready default-constructor plan. Defaulted definitions now
   prepare those direct dependencies; default selection walks defaulted child
   dependencies iteratively, with ready-plan reuse and active identity cycle
   detection. User-provided external declarations retain their demand boundary.
   Nontrivial recursive construction remains explicitly unfinished.
5. **Union rejection location/order.** For `S(): second(1), first(2)`, the old
   implementation diagnosed the second written initializer. The C++ owner
   diagnoses the second designated member in declaration order. The planning
   map now borrows original mem-initializers, retains their name locations and
   visits the member vector in declaration order. No hash iteration chooses
   the diagnostic. Duplicate-member checking and ordinary action order remain
   unchanged.
6. **Trivial subobject exception facts.** A trivial member constructor with
   an explicit `noexcept(false)` was omitted from the enclosing implicit or
   first-defaulted constructor's exception calculation, incorrectly emitting
   `unwind=no`. Plans now combine base/member constructor specifications and
   initializer throw facts. Explicit specifications use the existing deferred
   semantic query; implicit child plans supply their retained `nothrow` fact.
   Lowering omits trivial calls while consuming the correct enclosing metadata.

Seven new self-checking controls belong to PA11's executed `tests/controls/`
lane: `audit-static-member-object-effects.cpp`,
`audit-aggregate-array-string-helper.cpp`,
`audit-static-member-function-argument.cpp`,
`audit-static-bound-overload-argument.cpp`,
`audit-defaulted-trivial-member-plans.cpp`,
`audit-union-constructor-diagnostic-order.cpp` and
`audit-trivial-subobject-constructor-exceptions.cpp`.
All run through the existing course control checker and protected adapter;
no frozen fixture, reference, manifest or gate policy was edited.

## Ownership, demand and completeness review

The plain-storage group computes layout with class-owned ready/in-progress
states and an explicit dependency worklist; failed layout work restores its
active state. Trivial default plans retain canonical class/member IDs and
const/deletion/access facts. The accepted constructor subset rejects required
nontrivial child actions before publishing a supported enclosing plan; its
triviality predicate is not evidence of completed recursive construction.
Helper eligibility, brace elision, references, omitted values, arrays, unions,
volatile stores and self-reference sequencing were traced through the actual
original expression/member facts and final addresses, including the differing
bare/braced string paths. Typed `(class, count, cv)` helper keys and a separate
first-demand vector retain identity and deterministic order.

Receiver analysis uses indexed lookup, arity filtering, reusable candidate
scratch, canonical object cv/ref sequences and static-candidate neutrality.
The original ExprId facts retain the selected binding, receiver ExprId/source,
conversions and naming class. Scratch is cleared on Result paths. Member
initializer object context is narrowly saved/restored on success and failure.
Constructor actions retain original Initializers and declaration-ordered
EntityIds; the typed ConstructorId store is private. Canonical callable and
presentation signatures are distinct, without AST copying or text reparsing.
The cloned parameter vector in presentation signature construction represents
that distinct interned signature; it is not a cloned semantic body.

Lowering reads these facts without lookup, constructor selection or exception
redecision. Constructor entries use `(ConstructorId, Complete/Base)`, ordinary
entries EntityIds; deduplicated first demand, explicit C2/C1 order and C2 aliases
retain distinct ABI identities. Scalar/reference actions fit/bind the value
before member addressing; aggregate/array actions preserve their recorded
address-before-value order and recomputed `this` paths. Exact signed/unsigned,
floating and pointer conversions remain in the existing owners. Rejection
probes compare exit and diagnostic bytes, including the frozen non-list array
member restriction, access/deletion precedence and original union locations.

Each function/helper owns its builder, slots, temporaries, blocks and address
path scratch, is streamed once and releases its LowIR body. Program symbols,
aliases and the shared startup body retain only typed LowIR/name metadata;
frontend scopes and helper memo tables remain per unit. Reviewed maps use
compact identities; source/member vectors and explicit queues determine output
order. No unrelated global scan, duplicate parser, semantic-body cloning,
mechanical split, unsafe path, compiler/oracle delegation, new dependency,
lint suppression or environment read was introduced. The diff leaves
`dev/src/opt/`, `dev/src/native/`, tool entry names and Cargo policies unchanged.
There is no PA34 source/object mapping obligation at this PA11 checkpoint.

## Completed constructor-action group and architecture review

A private, unit-owned `ConstructorId` identifies each selected plan. It retains
canonical callable and dump presentation TypeIds separately, original member
EntityId/Initializer actions in declaration order, the hidden object pointer,
base/subobject IDs and nonthrowing facts. The source's special definition DeclId
and BodyId remain separate. First-declaration-defaulted constructors without a
written exception specification consume their subobject plan's exception fact;
an explicit specification, including `noexcept(false)`, takes precedence.

Default member initializers analyze with their canonical unqualified class
object context; that narrow context is restored on both success and failure.
Earlier fields, references, implicit calls and `this` use ordinary dense
original-expression receiver facts. Initializer throw queries reuse the existing
worklist and selected call/default facts; aggregate list prvalues visit their
required destructor edges. No name lookup or exception redecision occurs in
lowering. General constructor/temporary exception queries remain a boundary.

Local default construction consumes its selected plan and original default
arguments, with the object address before argument effects. Scalar/reference
constructor actions evaluate their value/binding before recomputing the member
address. Aggregate member and scalar-array actions use a typed `This` root:
each store recomputes the original path, with address-before-value order for
aggregate fields/array elements, exact pointer reloads, union untyped storage,
volatile memory facts, omitted zeros and nested reference bindings. Empty
parenthesized array initialization remains its original value-initialization
shape. Braced character-array data reuses the existing array lowering path.

The C++ `initialize_scalar_member` owner explicitly rejects a non-list array
member expression. That diagnostic is preserved, with the written body's
compound location or implicit body's zero location. Valid-language string-array
examples in `probes/character-array-default.cpp` and
`probes/character-array-parenthesized.cpp` reproduce this frozen lowering
limitation; both tools reject with identical status/diagnostics. No reference or
checker defect/instability was found and no contract was silently widened.

Lowering schedules `Entry::Function(EntityId)` or
`Entry::Constructor(ConstructorId, Complete/Base)` in one first-demand queue.
Explicit out-of-class definitions emit C2 before C1; demanded inline/implicit
C1 entries retain a separate C2 ABI alias. Already emitted base bodies suppress
that alias. Constructor ordinals follow the class's declaration vector. External
constructors emit typed signatures; inline definitions coalesce across units,
while unnamed-namespace constructors retain separate internal symbols, exact
object_root flags and local-object collision facts. ABI identities come from
canonical facts and typed graph terminals, never mangled-string reconstruction.

Implicit entries have no fabricated function EntityId or semantic AST. The
shared function builder owns hidden-object/source-parameter slots, locals,
blocks and temporaries and releases the body after streaming. Alias metadata
outlives that body in the typed program. All source paths stay parsed once;
there is no second semantic tree, text transport, whole-program polling,
subprocess implementation, unsafe/FFI code or new optimization. Optimizer,
register allocator and native backend sources are unchanged. Constructor and
ordinary function emission remain distinct from semantic analysis/demand.

destruction, global lifetime and local ABI boundaries rather than silent stubs.

Next port **general constructor selection and initialization-use facts** from
`sema/analyzer_calls.cpp`, `sema/analyzer_initialization.cpp`,
`sema/analyzer_construction.cpp` and `lower/object_lowering.cpp`. Record selected
constructors and all supplied/default argument conversions once on the original
initialization use, then let this entry pipeline consume them. Include direct,
copy and list initialization, explicit/deleted/access precedence, non-first
argument ranking and namespace/local functional forms. Representative residuals:
`pa11/tests/general/100-inline-constructor-copy-init.t`,
`pa11/tests/general/200-constructor-overload-default-arg-nonfirst-argument.t`,
`pa11/tests/general/200-external-ctor-overload-nonfirst-argument.t`,
`pa11/tests/spec/200-direct-list-init-explicit-ctor.t`.

Then extend the retained base/subobject IDs to recursive class/array actions
with EH/lifetime cleanup. No base path or lifecycle may be synthesized by
lowering lookup. Keep distinct complete/base identities, first-demand order,
original initializer actions, per-function cleanup ownership and exact exception
metadata; preserve optimizer/code-generator behavior throughout.

Earliest reported residual clusters include 25 bit-field declarations, 23
converting/derived initializations, 22 extended declaration specifiers, 14
nonvirtual base layouts, 11 written base constructor plans, eight nonaggregate
constructor list initializations, seven local ABI scopes and six storage
lifecycle lowerings. The 48 raw diagnostic/artifact mismatches still need their
owner-specific refinement as those groups are ported; `stage-summary.json`
retains every exact cluster identity and receipt. Other due groups remain
recursive construction/destruction, base layout/tail padding and inherited
receiver/access, operators/ADL/friends, static/class/local symbols, global/TLS/
block-static lifetimes, lexical/EH/goto cleanup, bit-fields, packing/alignment,
anonymous injection, inherited constructors, pseudo-destructors and
aggregate/value materialization/zeroinit boundaries. Copy/move/value transfers,
templates and virtual machinery retain their later-stage handout boundaries.
No full-stage completeness claim follows from this checkpoint.
